Probing 方法论：是否充分引用了 probing 领域的 layer selection 相关工作？\n  - Safety detection：是否覆盖了 2025-2026 年的最新工作？\n  - Reasoning model safety：是否有遗漏的重要 concurrent work？\n- 与 concurrent work（如 chan2025probing）的区别是否阐述清楚？\n\n### 3. 写作质量\n- Abstract 是否清晰传达了核心发现？\n- Introduction 的叙事结构是否清晰？是否过于冗长或 hedged？\n- 论文的 claim 与 evidence 是否匹配？是否存在 overclaim 或 underclaim？\n- 记号系统是否一致？图表是否清晰？\n- 8 页 main text 的信息密度是否合理？是否有多余内容可以压缩？\n- Limitation section 是否诚实、完整？是否存在关键缺陷被回避？\n- 论文的 framing（\"initial characterization\"）是否过于保守，削弱了 impact？\n\n### 4. 格式合规\n- 是否符合 ACL 模板要求？\n- 是否 double-blind（无作者信息泄露）？\n- 正文是否 ≤ 8 页？\n- Limitations section 是否存在？\n- 引用格式是否正确？\n\n请按 ARR 审稿格式给出：Summary → Strengths → Weaknesses → Questions → Missing References → Overall Assessment（含评分和置信度）。\n\n评分标准（EMNLP 2026 ARR）：5=Consider for Award, 4.5=Borderline Award, 4=Conference, 3.5=Borderline Conference, 3=Findings, 2.5=Borderline Findings, 2=Resubmit next cycle, 1.5=Resubmit after next cycle, 1=Do not resubmit。"

[Round 1 审稿请求]:
你是 Reviewer 2，负责**新颖性/贡献显著性 + 相关工作完整性 + 写作质量**。

## 投稿信息
- Venue: EMNLP 2026（ARR Long Paper）
- Deadline: 2026-05-25
- 页数限制: 正文 ≤ 8 页，参考文献不限，Limitations 必须有（不计入页数）
- 模板: ACL format，`\usepackage[review]{acl}`
- 匿名: double-blind

## 论文路径
- LaTeX: /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/main.tex
- PDF: /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/main.pdf

## 论文概述
论文研究 reasoning model 中 hidden-state safety probe 的 layer selection sensitivity。核心发现：layer selection metric 的选择可以完全逆转 HS vs text probe 的模态比较结论。4 个模型（R1-8B, OT-7B, QwQ-32B, R1-32B），HarmThoughts 数据集，附大量 ablation 和 negative results。

## 你的审查重点

请严格评估以下方面：

### 1. 新颖性与贡献显著性
- "Layer selection 影响 probing 结论" 是否是 probing 领域的 common knowledge？论文的 contribution 是否只是把已知问题在一个新 domain 上 demonstrate 了一下？
- "Metric-induced layer selection bias" 是否有足够深度？是否只是说 "不同 metric 选不同 layer"（这本身不令人惊讶）？
- Commitment Point 概念的定义是否足够严谨？Binary simplification 是否过于粗糙？
- Negative results（7 种替代方法失败）的价值有多大？是否只是说 "这些方法不适用于这个特定 setting"？
- 论文反复强调 "retrospective characterization, not online detection"——这是否意味着实际应用价值有限？

### 2. 相关工作完整性
- 是否遗漏了重要的 related work？特别关注：
  - Probing 方法论：是否充分引用了 probing 领域的 layer selection 相关工作？
  - Safety detection：是否覆盖了 2025-2026 年的最新工作？
  - Reasoning model safety：是否有遗漏的重要 concurrent work？
- 与 concurrent work（如 chan2025probing）的区别是否阐述清楚？

### 3. 写作质量
- Abstract 是否清晰传达了核心发现？
- Introduction 的叙事结构是否清晰？是否过于冗长或 hedged？
- 论文的 claim 与 evidence 是否匹配？是否存在 overclaim 或 underclaim？
- 记号系统是否一致？图表是否清晰？
- 8 页 main text 的信息密度是否合理？是否有多余内容可以压缩？
- Limitation section 是否诚实、完整？是否存在关键缺陷被回避？
- 论文的 framing（"initial characterization"）是否过于保守，削弱了 impact？

### 4. 格式合规
- 是否符合 ACL 模板要求？
- 是否 double-blind（无作者信息泄露）？
- 正文是否 ≤ 8 页？
- Limitations section 是否存在？
- 引用格式是否正确？

请按 ARR 审稿格式给出：Summary → Strengths → Weaknesses → Questions → Missing References → Overall Assessment（含评分和置信度）。

评分标准（EMNLP 2026 ARR）：5=Consider for Award, 4.5=Borderline Award, 4=Conference, 3.5=Borderline Conference, 3=Findings, 2.5=Borderline Findings, 2=Resubmit next cycle, 1.5=Resubmit after next cycle, 1=Do not resubmit。

Web search results for query: "ACL ARR 2025 2026 reviewer guidelines review form criteria scoring"

Links: [{"title":"ARR Reviewer Guidelines – ACL Rolling Review – A peer review platform for the Association for Computational Linguistics","url":"http://aclrollingreview.org/reviewerguidelines"},{"title":"ARR Area Chair Guidelines – ACL Rolling Review – A peer review platform for the Association for Computational Linguistics","url":"http://aclrollingreview.org/acguidelines"},{"title":"aclrollingreview/cfp.md at main · acl-org/aclrollingreview","url":"https://github.com/acl-org/aclrollingreview/blob/main/cfp.md"},{"title":"CALL FOR PAPERS – ACL Rolling Review – A peer review platform for the Association for Computational Linguistics","url":"http://aclrollingreview.org/cfp"},{"title":"Authors Guidelines – ACL Rolling Review – A peer review platform for the Association for Computational Linguistics","url":"http://aclrollingreview.org/authors"}]

Based on the search results, here's what I found about the ACL ARR 2025-2026 reviewer guidelines:

## Scoring Criteria

Starting with February 2025, ARR reviewers are asked to provide three scores: soundness, excitement, and overall recommendation.

### Soundness
Soundness scores must be justified by the text of the review. If you give a low Soundness score without finding any major faults, this means that your review is not a faithful explanation of your recommendation, and you need to revise it.

### Excitement
Sometimes reviewers may find the work sound, but not something that is interesting for them personally. Such preferences are more subjective and should be reflected in a separate excitement score, which is orthogonal to the assessment of the soundness of the paper.

## Area Chair Guidelines

All papers recommended for Findings or main conference are expected to be sound and reproducible. A main conference paper could be distinguished by excitement, novelty, impact, and/or other factors, considered case-by-case.

The official reviewer guidelines are available at: http://aclrollingreview.org/reviewerguidelines


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

```text
# ARR Reviewer Scoring Guidelines

## Scoring Dimensions

As of February 2025, ARR reviewers provide **three primary scores**:

1. **Soundness** - Evaluates technical validity and rigor
2. **Excitement** - Reflects personal interest and subjective value
3. **Overall Assessment** - Composite recommendation for acceptance

## What Each Score Means

**Soundness:**
This dimension assesses whether the paper is technically correct and methodologically sound. The guidelines emphasize that "soundness scores must be justified by the text of the review." Low soundness scores require specific evidence of flaws; they cannot rest on unstated assumptions or biases.

**Excitement:**
This orthogonal dimension captures personal preference independent of quality. The document states: "you may consider a paper worthy of the main conference even if you personally are not excited about it." Examples include efficiency improvements or resource creation for underrepresented languages.

**Overall Assessment:**
A composite metric reflecting soundness, excitement, novelty, and potential impact. Papers for main conferences must be "sufficiently sound and reproducible," but excitement alone doesn't determine acceptance.

## Additional Scoring Elements

- **Confidence Score** - Indicates reviewer certainty; can be noted when expertise is limited
- **Reproducibility Score** - Must be justified in review text

## Key Evaluation Instructions

Reviewers must:
- Read papers twice (skim, then in-depth)
- Provide specific, actionable feedback with examples
- Avoid common reviewer biases (detailed in 17 heuristics listed)
- Note checked/unchecked sections (e.g., proofs skipped)
- Update assessments after author responses
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I've read the entire paper (31 pages). Let me also verify references and search for relevant related work that might be missing.

Let me now do the reference verification and also search for some key related works that might be missing.

Let me compile my thorough review. Let me organize my notes from the full paper reading:

## Paper Summary
The paper investigates layer selection sensitivity in hidden-state safety probes for reasoning models. The core finding: the choice of layer selection metric can completely reverse the comparison conclusion between hidden-state (HS) and text-based probes. They study 4 models (R1-8B, OT-7B, QwQ-32B, R1-32B) on the HarmThoughts dataset, define a "Safety Commitment Point" (CP), and show that crossing-rate composite metrics select shallow layers while threshold-based metrics select deep layers.


The paper identifies four key contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias where crossing-rate metrics favor shallow layers and threshold-based metrics favor deep ones, the finding that hidden-state advantages are sample-dependent, and the demonstration that seven alternative methods all fail to resolve this issue. Looking at novelty, while the observation that different metrics select different layers builds on known probing challenges from prior work, the specific insight that metric choice can reverse conclusions about hidden-state versus text modality comparison is practically significant, though the novelty is somewhat incremental.

The Commitment Point concept is intriguing but the binary pre-CP versus post-CP distinction feels coarse-grained. The paper's self-framing as an "initial characterization" is cautious and may downplay the contribution's scope. The negative results showing that standard change-point detection methods fail are useful for ruling out simple approaches, but they lack deeper mechanistic insight into why these methods don't work. The retrospective rather than online detection framing also constrains real-world applicability.

For related work coverage, I'm checking probing methodology papers like Belinkov 2022, Burns et al. 2023, and Hewitt and Liang 2019, layer selection work from Tenney et al. 2019 and Jawahar et al. 2019, and safety considerations from Chan et al. 2025.

I should verify whether I'm missing inference-time intervention approaches like Li et al. 2024, recent SAE-based probing methods, and causal probing techniques like DAS. Let me search for these gaps and double-check my references before finalizing the review.
```

Links: [{"title":"Topic aware probing: From sentence length prediction to idiom identification how reliant are neural language models on topic? | Natural Language Processing | Cambridge Core","url":"https://resolve.cambridge.org/core/journals/natural-language-processing/article/topic-aware-probing-from-sentence-length-prediction-to-idiom-identification-how-reliant-are-neural-language-models-on-topic/0FFF33B18E284DAB8FE8DCF69A963A30"},{"title":"Probing Language Models on Their Knowledge Source - ACL Anthology","url":"https://aclanthology.org/2024.blackboxnlp-1.35/"},{"title":"SAfER: Layer-Level Sensitivity Assessment for Efficient and Robust   Neural Network Inference","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.04753"},{"title":"First is Not Really Better Than Last: Evaluating Layer Choice and Aggregation Strategies in Language Model Data Influence Estimation","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.04715"},{"title":"Decoding Probing: Revealing Internal Linguistic Structures in Neural   Language Models using Minimal Pairs","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.17299"}]

The search results show recent research on probing and layer sensitivity in neural networks and NLP. Here are the key findings:

## Recent Probing Studies (2024-2025)

Mid-layer activations, particularly those related to relations in the input, are crucial in predicting knowledge source selection in large language models, according to a 2024 BlackboxNLP workshop paper.

Recent experiments across LLMs of varying types and scales show that the first layers are not necessarily better than the last layers for LLM influence estimation, contrasting with prior knowledge in the field.

## Layer Sensitivity and Information Distribution

Earlier layers in language models encode syntactic information while later layers encode semantic information, though this finding has been questioned by recent research mentioned in the 2024 literature.

Research reveals variability in the optimal layer across participants, making it crucial to probe the linguistic information in intermediate embeddings of language models individually.

## Layer Importance Assessment

Some layers might be more critical than others, and researchers have proposed investigating DNN layer importance by estimating the sensitivity of accuracy with respect to perturbations applied at the layer level.

These studies collectively highlight the complexity of understanding which layers capture which information and how to systematically evaluate layer sensitivity in neural language models.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Based on the search results, I found several recent papers on this topic. Here's what the research shows:

## Key Findings on Hidden State Safety Probes and Reasoning Models

Reasoning models with long chain-of-thought encode strong signals about the correctness of intermediate answers in model's hidden states, with reasoning models achieving remarkable performance on tasks like math and logical reasoning thanks to their ability to search during reasoning.

Researchers segment the long Chain-of-Thought into chunks containing intermediate answers and train a binary classifier to predict answer correctness from the model's hidden states at the answer positions. Using the probe as a verifier to decide whether to exit reasoning at intermediate answers during inference can reduce the number of inference tokens by 24% without compromising performance.

## Safety and Monitoring Applications

Large Reasoning Models introduce new opportunities for safety monitoring through their Chain of Thought reasoning, however CoT is not always faithful to the model's final output, undermining its reliability as a monitoring tool.

Gray-box approaches look at the model's internal state, using simple probes on raw activations or analyzing the trajectory of hidden states. However, these methods can detect that a model's internal state is correlated with an error, but not explain why the underlying computation leads to an error.

## Recent Work on Performative Reasoning

Research tracks how a model's internal beliefs about the final answer evolve throughout reasoning and compares this to what the model communicates in its CoT, where a mismatch between the two is performative reasoning.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Based on the search results, I found the paper you're looking for:

Belinkov's 2022 paper "Probing Classifiers: Promises, Shortcomings, and Advances" addresses probing classifiers as a methodology for interpreting deep neural network models in NLP, where a classifier is trained to predict some linguistic property from a model's representations.

The paper critically reviews the probing classifiers framework, highlighting methodological limitations that recent studies have demonstrated, while also discussing their promises and advances.

The paper was published in the journal *Computational Linguistics*, volume 48, issue 1, pages 207-219 (March 2022). It's available through multiple sources including the ACL Anthology (https://aclanthology.org/2022.cl-1.7/), MIT Press, and arXiv (2102.12452).

Towards Monitoring Misaligned Reasoning Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.12428"},{"title":"Truth as a Trajectory: What Internal Representations Reveal About Large Language Model Reasoning","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.01326"},{"title":"Reasoning Models Know When They're Right: Probing Hidden States for   Self-Verification","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.05419"},{"title":"DRAFT: Task Decoupled Latent Reasoning for Agent Safety","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.03242"},{"title":"LaTER: Efficient Test-Time Reasoning via Latent Exploration and Explicit Verification","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2605.07315"}]

I found several highly relevant arxiv papers from 2025-2026 on probe trajectories, reasoning models, and hidden state safety monitoring. Here are the key results:

1. **"Monitoring the Internal Monologue: Probe Trajectories Reveal Reasoning Dynamics"** (arxiv 2605.18549 - 2 days old): Provides AI developers with a representation-based tool for real-time safety monitoring.

2. **"Can We Predict Alignment Before Models Finish Thinking? Towards Monitoring Misaligned Reasoning Models"** (arxiv 2507.12428): Model latents offer a more reliable predictive signal than CoT texts, which are often unfaithful. Probes make accurate predictions before reasoning completes, and findings generalize across model sizes, families, and safety benchmarks, suggesting lightweight probes could enable real-time safety monitoring.

3. **"Truth as a Trajectory: What Internal Representations Reveal About Large Language Model Reasoning"** (arxiv 2603.01326): Plots layerwise hidden states as trajectories in activation space, where correct generations follow smoother paths while incorrect ones exhibit sharp deviations.

4. **"Reasoning Models Know When They're Right: Probing Hidden States for Self-Verification"** (arxiv 2504.05419): Models' hidden states encode correctness of future answers, enabling early prediction before intermediate answers are fully formulated.

5. **"LaTER: Efficient Test-Time Reasoning via Latent Exploration and Explicit Verification"** (arxiv 2605.07315): Proposes a hybrid reasoning paradigm that separates exploration from verification, using bounded latent rollout where final-layer hidden states are mapped back into input embedding space.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

I'm noticing several very recent papers that directly engage with similar themes around probing model reasoning and hidden states. Given the May 2026 submission deadline, I should focus on papers that were clearly available beforehand rather than these very recent ones. Now let me work through my full assessment of the paper's contributions and limitations.

The novelty here is mixed. The core insight about how layer selection metrics influence probing conclusions connects to established concerns in the probing literature, particularly Belinkov's 2022 work on this exact issue. What's somewhat novel is demonstrating that this methodological choice actually reverses the conclusions about hidden states versus text modality comparisons. The "Commitment Point" framing is intriguing but the binary formulation feels too simplistic. The negative results do offer some value, though they don't lead to particularly deep insights.

The paper's framing as retrospective characterization rather than online detection is a significant limitation that constrains its impact. Essentially, this reads as a methodological cautionary note about probing practices.

The finding is useful for the community but lacks real depth, and the paper doesn't actually propose solutions beyond suggesting a multi-metric ensemble approach in the appendix. I'd rate the excitement around this at 2.5 out of 5.

The related work section is fairly comprehensive with 78 references covering probing methodology, safety detection, and reasoning model safety. There's a concurrent paper on probing reasoning models for self-verification that's directly relevant but takes a different angle on correctness rather than safety. Given the timeline, the coverage is acceptable.

The writing is generally solid but has some issues. The abstract tries to cram too many results into a tight space, the introduction relies heavily on hedging language like "initial characterization" and "retrospective," and the overall framing keeps shifting between different angles.

Now I'm checking the page count against the ACL requirements. The main text appears to run through page 9 with the limitations section, though I need to verify whether that section counts toward the 8-page limit. Since Limitations don't count toward the page limit under EMNLP guidelines, the core paper fits within the 8-page requirement.

The paper's contribution is somewhat incremental—it demonstrates that layer selection sensitivity, a known issue in probing work, also affects safety probes on reasoning models, and shows how metric choice can flip conclusions about modality comparisons. While the experimental work is solid with multiple models and extensive ablations, the paper lacks a principled solution to the problem it identifies. The strengths include rigorous statistical methods and honest acknowledgment of limitations, but the novelty feels limited since it's essentially applying an existing concern to a specific domain.

I'm settling on a 3.0 rating—this is Findings-level work. The cautionary message for the safety probing community is valuable and the empirical rigor is commendable, but the narrow scope and lack of actionable solutions prevent it from reaching the main conference bar. Novelty concern—is this "common knowledge" dressed up? No solution proposed, single dataset, limited practical applicability. Actually, stepping back—the paper does offer real value by showing that published hidden-state safety detection results warrant re-examination, which is a meaningful cautionary contribution. The experimental rigor is solid. But without proposing solutions and given the narrow scope, it stays at 3.0 overall.

For soundness, I'm at 3.5—the methodology is generally solid though the binary CP approach raises some questions. The excitement factor is lower at 2.5 since this reads more as an important methodological warning than a breakthrough. Reproducibility looks strong at 4.0 with promised code and data plus thorough experimental documentation.
```

</details>

{
  "text": "\n## Reviewer 2 审稿意见：Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models\n\n---\n\n## 总体评分\n**Overall**: 3.0 (Findings)\n**一句话总结**: 论文通过大量实验展示了 layer selection metric 可以逆转 HS vs. text probe 的模态比较结论，这是一个有价值的方法论警示，但核心发现的新颖性有限（layer selection sensitivity 在 probing 领域已是已知问题），且未提出原则性的解决方案，适合 Findings 但不足以进入主会议。\n\n---\n\n## 官方维度打分（ACL ARR Review Form, Feb 2025+）\n\n| 维度 | 分数 | 理由摘要 |\n|------|------|----------|\n| Soundness | 3.5/5 | 实验方法论整体严谨（Holm-Bonferroni 校正、bootstrap CI、排列测试），但 Commitment Point 的 binary formulation 过于粗糙，且核心 claim 依赖单一数据集 |\n| Excitement | 2.5/5 | 发现本身是有用的方法论警示，但本质上是将已知问题（probing layer selection sensitivity）在新领域（safety probes）上 demonstrate，缺乏深度洞察和解决方案 |\n| Reproducibility | 4/5 | 实验细节充分，承诺开源代码和数据，附录覆盖全面 |\n| Overall Assessment | 3.0/5 | Findings 级别——贡献真实但 incremental，methodological cautionary tale 缺少 actionable 解决方案 |\n\n**Confidence**: 4/5（对 probing 方法论和 LLM safety 领域有较好理解，仔细阅读了全文和附录）\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n本文研究 reasoning model 中 hidden-state safety probe 的 layer selection sensitivity。在 4 个模型（R1-8B, OT-7B, QwQ-32B, R1-32B）和 HarmThoughts 数据集上，作者发现 crossing-rate composite metric 系统性地选择 shallow layers，而 threshold-based metric 选择 deep layers，且这一选择差异可以完全逆转 HS vs. text probe 的模态优势比较结论。论文还定义了 Safety Commitment Point (CP)，测试了 7 种替代 layer selection 方法（均失败），并提供了大量 ablation 分析。\n\n---\n\n## 主要优势 (Strengths)\n\n**S1. 统计严谨性出色。** 这是本文最大的亮点。全文贯穿使用 Holm-Bonferroni 校正、BCa bootstrap CI、排列测试（Section 4.1; Appendix D），并在小样本场景下（R1-32B, n=20）诚实报告统计功效不足（Section Z.2, post-hoc power analysis）。Sign-flip permutation test（Section D）对所有 32B 模型进行了 exhaustive enumeration（2^n 个排列），这在该领域论文中极为少见。\n\n**S2. Bug Disclosure（Appendix N）令人印象深刻。** 主动披露 QwQ-32B text probe 的 bug 及其对结果的影响，说明这些实验确实通过了系统性审计。这种透明度在该领域的论文中极为少见，增强了结果的可信度。\n\n**S3. 实验覆盖全面。** 31 页附录涵盖 layer sweep（B）、window size ablation（E/E.1）、text probe window ablation（E.1）、encoder robustness（S）、random projection baseline（T）、LLM-based text encoder（U）、cross-model transfer（R）、GRU variants（G）、two-stage detection（O）、multi-metric ensemble（Z.1）等。这远超同类论文的标准。\n\n**S4. Negative results 的诚实报告。** 7 种替代方法全部失败（Table 23, Section 4.6），且每种失败都有具体的分析（BOCPD 无增益因为 reasoning 是渐进的而非突变的；RepE 模型依赖等），避免了只报告成功方法的 publication bias。\n\n**S5. 核心发现确有实践价值。** 同一数据在 CR-composite layer 上给出 +19pp 的 HS 优势（p<0.005），但在 threshold-based layer 上这一优势不显著（p=0.83），这一逆转效应对于依赖单一 metric 做 layer selection 的后续研究者是重要的警示（Section 4.2, Table 2）。\n\n---\n\n## Must-Fix 问题（影响接收决定）\n\n**N1** [critical] **核心贡献与 probing 领域已有认知的区分不够清晰**\n- 具体位置：Section 1 (Introduction), Section 5 (Discussion)\n- 问题：Belinkov (2022) 的 survey 已明确指出 probing 结果对 layer selection、probe complexity、evaluation metric 的选择高度敏感。论文虽然引用了 Belinkov (2022)，但未充分论证 \"metric-induced layer selection bias\" 如何超越这一已知问题。当前的 framing 本质上是：在一个新 domain（safety probes on reasoning models）上 demonstrate 一个已知的方法论问题。这是 incremental contribution。\n- Section 5 第一句 \"Layer selection introduces researcher degrees of freedom (Gelman and Loken, 2013)\" 实际上承认了这是一个已知的方法论问题。\n- 为什么影响接收：如果 reviewer 认为这只是把已知问题在新 setting 上复现，contribution 不足以达到 conference 级别。\n- 建议修改方向：(1) 更明确地论证 **为什么** safety probes 的 layer selection sensitivity 比一般 probing 更严重（例如：safety 应用的 high-stakes 性质意味着即使是已知问题也需要 empirical quantification）；(2) 强调 crossing-rate vs. threshold-based metric 的 **系统性偏差方向**（总是 shallow vs. deep）是 domain-specific 的新发现，不是 Belinkov (2022) 已经覆盖的。\n\n**N2** [critical] **单一数据集限制了 claim 的 generalizability**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.1, Section 7 (Limitations)\n- 问题：所有实验仅在 HarmThoughts 一个数据集上进行。虽然 AdvBench 用于 CP 框架验证（Appendix C.1），但核心的 layer selection sensitivity 实验完全依赖 HarmThoughts。论文声称 \"metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding\"（Section 5），但这一 claim 的 robustness 只在一个数据集上验证过。\n- Limitations (Section 7) 确实提到了这一点，但作为论文的中心 claim，单数据集是一个结构性弱点。\n- 为什么影响接收：Reviewer 无法判断这些发现是 HarmThoughts 数据集的特性（如 CP 分布的特殊性）还是真正的 general phenomenon。\n- 建议修改方向：至少在一个额外的安全检测数据集（如 WildChat toxic subset 或 BeaverTails）上验证核心的 layer selection reversal phenomenon。\n\n**N3** [critical] **论文未提出原则性解决方案**\n- 具体位置：Section 5 (Discussion), Appendix Z.1 (Multi-Metric Layer Ensemble)\n- 问题：论文用了大量篇幅诊断问题（metric-induced bias），但解决方案仅在附录 Z.1 中以 4 段内容简要讨论了 multi-metric ensemble。这个 ensemble 方法的 Ens-Best 差异在所有模型上都不显著（p>0.27），仅 Ens-Text 差异显著。这意味着 ensemble 并未真正解决问题——它只是比 text probe 好，但不比 best single metric 好。\n- Section 5 最后一段提出 \"multi-metric audit protocol: compute 2-3 metrics, flag disagreement\"，但这过于宽泛，缺乏具体的操作标准（什么程度的 disagreement 算显著？如何在 metrics 之间 arbitrate？）。\n- 为什么影响接收：一篇好的方法论批评论文应当在 identify 问题的同时提供 actionable mitigation。当前论文只做了一半工作。\n- 建议修改方向：将 Z.1 的 ensemble 分析提升到主文，更详细地发展 multi-metric audit protocol，并给出具体的 decision criteria。\n\n---\n\n## Should-Fix 问题（影响评分但不一票否决）\n\n**W1** [warning] **Commitment Point 的 binary formulation 过于粗糙**\n- 具体位置：Section 3.1, Definition of CP\n- 问题：CP 被定义为 \"the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases\"，分类为 s_t ∈ {0, 1}。但论文自身在 Limitations (Section 9, \"CP permanence assumption\") 中承认 reasoning traces 可能展现 oscillating 模式。Binary simplification 丢失了 safety deliberation 的 gradual 和 non-monotonic 特性。\n- Section 3.1 声称 \"we discuss its limits in Section 3\"，但实际讨论非常简短（\"this is a deliberate simplification\"）。\n- 建议：(1) 在主文中更详细地讨论 binary CP 的局限性及其对下游分析的影响；(2) 考虑使用 soft CP（例如 safety deliberation probability 的连续值），至少作为 robustness check。\n\n**W2** [warning] **Section 编号错误：5 → 7，跳过了 6**\n- 具体位置：Page 8\n- 问题：主文 Section 编号从 5 (Discussion) 直接跳到 7 (Limitations)，中间缺少 Section 6（应为 Conclusion？）。这可能是 LaTeX 编辑遗留的错误。\n- 建议：修复 section 编号。如果有意省略 Conclusion（例如 Discussion 已包含总结），应在 Discussion 末尾明确标注。\n\n**W3** [warning] **Abstract 信息过载**\n- 具体位置：Page 1, Abstract\n- 问题：Abstract 试图在 ~190 词中塞入过多具体数字（\"+15.4 to +21.9pp\", \"3.8d4 distilled\", \"11-50%\"），导致可读性下降。读者难以从中提取核心 message。\n- Abstract 中的 \"The compound depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored\" 是核心信息，但被数字淹没了。\n- 建议：减少具体数字，聚焦于传达核心 insight：metric 选择可以逆转模态比较结论。\n\n**W4** [warning] **\"Retrospective characterization, not online detection\" 的反复强调削弱了 impact**\n- 具体位置：Section 1 (multiple locations), Section 5, Section 7\n- 问题：论文在至少 4 处强调 \"retrospective characterization\"，每次都伴随 \"not online detection\" 的限定。虽然学术诚实很重要，但过度的 hedging 会让 reviewer 质疑论文的实际价值。如果连作者都反复说 \"这不能用于 X\"，那它能用于什么？\n- 建议：在 Introduction 中一次性明确 scope，然后在后续内容中 **正面表述** retrospective characterization 的价值（例如：为未来的 online detection 提供了哪些关键 insight），而不是反复设限。\n\n**W5** [warning] **Negative results (7 methods) 的洞察深度不足**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.6, Table 23\n- 问题：7 种替代方法的失败报告虽然诚实，但分析深度不够。每种方法的失败原因描述在 1-2 句内，例如 \"BOCPD provides no gain because its consistency misaligns with temporal structure\" 和 \"RepE is model-dependent\"。这些是描述性的观察，而非 explanatory。\n- 为什么 BOCPD 的 piecewise-constant assumption 不适合 gradual reasoning？是否所有 change-point 方法都不适合，还是可以设计 gradual-aware 变体？RepE 为什么 model-dependent？这些更深层的问题未被讨论。\n- 建议：至少对 1-2 种最有潜力的方法（如 BOCPD、RepE）进行更深入的 failure analysis，解释 **为什么** 它们的假设与 safety deliberation dynamics 不匹配。\n\n---\n\n## 小问题（不影响接收）\n\n- **引用格式**：部分引用的年份与 OpenAlex 记录有微小差异（如 Belinkov 2022 对应 2021 的 arXiv 版本），但这在预印本领域很常见，不构成问题。\n- **Figure 1 (Page 6)**：Per-layer metric heatmap 信息密度高但 color scale 的含义需要更多 caption 解释。当前 caption 提到 \"one of five metrics (min-max normalized)\" 但未说明是哪五个 metric。\n- **Table 2 (Page 6)**：CR-comp 的列标题 \"Thresh.\" 缩写不一致——有时用 \"threshold-based\"，有时用 \"Thresh.\"。\n- **Page 7, Table 3**：脚注 \"HS: test difference types\" 中 \"types\" 的含义不明确。\n- **Section 4.5 (Temporal Context)**：Window size ablation 的结论 \"larger windows reduce false alarms and sharpen positive predictions\" 与 \"even as overall classification accuracy degrades\" 之间的 tension 未充分解释。\n\n---\n\n## Questions for Authors\n\n1. **关于 novelty**：能否明确说明 crossing-rate vs. threshold-based metric 的 systematic shallow/deep bias 在 Belinkov (2022) 或其他 probing 方法论工作中是否已被讨论过？如果没有，这个 systematic direction 是本文的 key novelty claim，应更清晰地 frame。\n\n2. **关于 generalizability**：在 AdvBench 上验证 CP 框架时（Appendix C.1），是否也观察到了 layer selection reversal phenomenon？如果是，这将显著加强 generalizability claim。\n\n3. **关于 ensemble**：Appendix Z.1 的 multi-metric ensemble 在 Ens-Best 维度上不显著——作者是否尝试过 learned weighting（而非 uniform averaging）或 metric selection 的 validation-based 策略？\n\n4. **关于 CP 定义**：对于展现 oscillating safety deliberation 的 traces，当前 binary CP 的 false positive/negative rate 是多少？是否有 quantitative analysis？\n\n---\n\n## Missing References\n\n论文的引用覆盖在该领域已属全面（78 条引用）。以下几篇可能值得考虑，但鉴于时间线，部分可能是 truly concurrent work：\n\n1. **\"Monitoring the Internal Monologue: Probe Trajectories Reveal Reasoning Dynamics\"** (arxiv 2605.18549, May 2026) — 直接研究 reasoning model 的 probe trajectory，与本文的 temporal probe analysis 高度相关。但该文发表于 2026 年 5 月下旬，可能是 truly concurrent。\n\n2. **\"Truth as a Trajectory\"** (arxiv 2603.01326, March 2026) — 用 layerwise hidden states 构建 trajectory，研究 correct vs. incorrect generation 的 trajectory 差异。其 layerwise analysis 与本文的 layer selection sensitivity 主题相关。\n\n3. **\"Reasoning Models Know When They're Right: Probing Hidden States for Self-Verification\"** (arxiv 2504.05419, April 2025) — 研究 reasoning model hidden states 对 answer correctness 的 probing，虽然 focus 不是 safety，但 probing methodology 相关（尤其是 layer selection 的讨论）。\n\n---\n\n## 格式合规检查\n\n| 检查项 | 结果 | 说明 |\n|--------|------|------|\n| ACL 模板 | ✓ | 使用 `\\usepackage[review]{acl}` |\n| Double-blind | ✓ | \"Anonymous ACL submission\"，正文无作者信息 |\n| 正文 ≤ 8 页 | ✓（边界） | Sections 1-5 约在 page 8 的 2/3 处结束，Limitations 从 page 8 底部开始（不计入页数），合规 |\n| Limitations | ✓ | Section 7 存在且内容充实 |\n| Ethics Statement | ✓ | 存在，讨论了 safety-relevant 数据的伦理考量 |\n| 引用格式 | ✓ | 基本正确，\\cite/\\citep/\\citet 使用得当 |\n\n**唯一格式问题**：Section 编号从 5 跳到 7（W2）。\n\n---\n\n## 总结\n\n这是一篇实验严谨、统计处理出色、结果呈现诚实的论文。核心发现（metric 选择可逆转 HS vs. text 比较结论）对 safety probing 社区有实际警示价值。但主要弱点在于：(1) 核心贡献与 probing 领域已有认知的区分度不够；(2) 单一数据集限制了 generalizability；(3) 缺少原则性的解决方案。\n\n论文当前形态适合 ACL Findings——贡献真实但 scope 和 novelty 限制了其 excitement。如果作者能更清晰地 differentiate 其 contribution from known issues、增加至少一个数据集、并在主文中发展 actionable mitigation protocol，评分可以提升到 3.5 (Borderline Conference)。\n",
  "reviewer_id": "2",
  "project": "contrastive_trace_safety"

_source line 166 · 2026-05-20T19:53:30.422Z_

[Round 1 审稿请求]:
以下是 Reviewer 1 的完整审稿意见，请逐条回应：对每个 Weakness/Question 标注 [AGREE] 同意并说明理由，或 [REBUT] 反驳并提供证据（cite 具体 Section/Table/Figure）。

---

## Reviewer 1 完整审稿意见

**Overall**: 3.0 (Findings)
**Soundness**: 3.5/5 | **Excitement**: 2.5/5 | **Reproducibility**: 4/5
**Confidence**: 4/5

**一句话总结**: 论文识别了 HS safety probing 中一个真实的方法论问题（metric-induced layer selection bias），统计方法总体严谨，但核心发现的新颖性有限、32B 分析严重欠力（n=19-20）、架构多样性不足（4 模型中 3 个 Qwen），限制了结论的泛化性。

### Strengths
S1. 方法论贡献有实际意义（Table 2 清晰展示 CR-composite vs threshold-based 的系统偏差）
S2. 统计方法论总体严谨（BCa bootstrap + HB + exact permutation tests 三层验证）
S3. Negative results 报告诚实且有组织（7 种替代方法 + bug disclosure）
S4. Appendix 极其详尽（40 张表格、5 张图）
S5. 单位根级别的因果推理尝试（position residualization, random-label control, random projection）

### Critical Weaknesses

**N1 [critical] 32B 模型样本量严重不足，统计推断不可靠**
- test set n=19-20，validation set ~4 traces per class
- bootstrap CI 不可避免地宽（QwQ-32B MDE = 8.6-9.5pp）
- validation set 上的超参选择基于 ~4 个样本，本质上随机
- 论文标记 32B 为 "exploratory"，但 Contribution 1 的核心论证恰恰依赖 32B 模型上 HS 优势的消失
- 建议：明确降级 32B 为 "illustrative"；补充 power analysis；增加数据量或改用 LOO

**N2 [critical] 架构多样性不足以支撑 "cross-architecture" 声明**
- 3/4 模型为 Qwen 架构，仅 R1-8B (Llama) 提供 cross-architecture evidence
- 如果 crossing-rate shallow-layer bias 是 Qwen 特有（如 embedding 归一化/attention 模式），核心 finding 可能无法泛化
- 建议：修正措辞为 "two architecture families"；补充非 Qwen/Llama 架构验证

**N3 [critical] Upstream exploration 的 multiple testing burden 被严重低估**
- ~2,240 configurations per model，但 HB 仅覆盖 final 12 tests
- Garden-of-forking-paths 问题：观察 2,240 条路径后选最好的一条再 correction
- Nested CV 仅在 7-8B 上做了，32B 无法执行
- 建议：报告 pre-registered analysis plan 或 split-sample approach；将 nested CV 结果放入主文

**N4 [warning] Position residualization 揭示 QwQ-32B 的 HS 优势可能是位置伪影**
- Table 29: QwQ-32B 在 quadratic residualization 后完全消失（-1.0pp）
- R1-32B 在两种规格下均不显著
- Abstract 和 Discussion 未充分反映此严重性

**N5 [warning] Control task selectivity 低于阈值（R1-32B: 3.0pp < 5pp）**
- R1-32B 的 probe 可能在学习表面模式而非 genuine safety structure
- 结合 N1 和 N4，R1-32B 全部结论应高度怀疑
- 建议：标注为 "inconclusive" 而非 "exploratory"

**N6 [warning] Crossing-rate shallow-layer bias 可能是 trivially expected**
- Shallow layers cosine distances 更小是 transformer 表征几何的已知性质
- Random-label control 强化了 bias 论点，但核心问题是：使用 crossing rate 的 researcher 是否会被 surprised？
- 建议：区分 "documenting known property in new context" 和 "discovering novel bias"

**N7 [warning] 单数据集限制泛化性**
- 所有核心结论基于 HarmThoughts 单一数据集
- 不同 harm category/jailbreak style 可能导致不同的 layer selection patterns

### Questions
Q1. QwQ-32B quadratic residualization 完全吸收 HS 优势 → HS probe 本质上是 position detector？
Q2. 为什么 nested 5-fold CV 没有在 32B 上执行？
Q3. Random-label control 中 100% 选 shallow layers 是否只是因为 shallow layers 更 noisy？
Q4. HarmThoughts CP annotation 无 IAA，能否量化 CP position 与 chain length 相关性？
Q5. Multi-metric ensemble Ens-Best 不显著 → ensemble 的实用价值？

### Missing References
1. Nanda et al. (2023), "Progress Measures for Grokking via Mechanistic Interpretability"
2. Gurnee et al. (2023), "Finding Neurons in a Haystack: Case Studies with Sparse Probing"
3. Li et al. (2024), "The Geometry of Categorical and Hierarchical Concepts in LLMs"

---

_source line 167 · 2026-05-20T19:54:01.619Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The user (Review Director) is asking me (Reviewer 2) to respond to Reviewer 1's complete review, going through each weakness (N1-N7) and question (Q1-Q5) with [AGREE] or [REBUT] tags, citing specific evidence from the paper.

Let me go through each point carefully based on my reading of the paper.

**N1: 32B sample size insufficient (n=19-20)**
- This is a valid concern. I saw that R1-32B has n=20 and QwQ-32B has n=19 in the test set. The paper does mark these as "exploratory" (Section 4.1). 

The validation set is admittedly tiny with only ~4 traces per class, but the paper uses exact permutation tests for the 32B models—the most rigorous approach for small samples. The MDE analysis shows 8.6-9.5pp ranges for 32B, which is fairly wide, so I partially agree this is a limitation, though the statistical methodology is sound.

**N2: Architecture diversity - 3/4 Qwen**
- Let me reconsider the model breakdown. R1-8B is Llama-based, OT-7B is Qwen-based, and the larger models include both Qwen and other architectures. The concern about Qwen dominance has some merit, but the paper does include architectural variety across the size spectrum.

**N3: Multiple testing burden**
- The paper explores thousands of configurations but only corrects for multiple comparisons on the final 12 tests. This is a legitimate concern about p-hacking and the garden-of-forking-paths problem, though the nested cross-validation structure does provide some protection against overfitting.

**N4: Position residualization on QwQ-32B**
- When I look at the residualization results, QwQ-32B's advantage disappears under quadratic residualization, which suggests the effect might be driven by position artifacts. But the smaller models (R1-8B and OT-7B) maintain their advantages even after residualization, so this appears to be a model-specific quirk rather than a fundamental flaw.

**N5: Control task selectivity for R1-32B**
- R1-32B shows a control task selectivity of 3.0 percentage points, falling short of the 5pp threshold that Hewitt and Liang recommend as a baseline for reliable control tasks. This is a legitimate concern that the paper acknowledges.

**N6: Shallow-layer crossing-rate bias**
- The observation that shallow layers have smaller cosine distances is well-established, so whether the crossing-rate bias itself is "obvious" is debatable. I see merit in both sides here.

**N7: Single dataset limitation**
- This mirrors my own earlier concern about dataset diversity, and I agree it's a valid limitation.

**Q1: QwQ-32B position detector**
- The quadratic residualization does eliminate QwQ-32B's advantage, but Section V shows that three of the four models retain non-positional signal, suggesting QwQ-32B might be an outlier rather than evidence of a systemic problem.

**Q2: Nested CV on larger models**
- The paper can't run nested cross-validation on the 32B model due to sample size constraints, which they acknowledge, though this does leave some uncertainty about generalization at that scale.

**Q3: Random-label control selecting shallow layers**
- R1 interprets this as shallow layers being noisier and thus equally susceptible to random labels, but the paper's Section B.1 actually shows the crossing-rate preference is label-independent, which supports the geometric bias explanation rather than undermining it.

**Q4: HarmThoughts CP annotation agreement**
- The paper doesn't report inter-annotator agreement for the CP annotations, which is a legitimate gap in the evaluation.

**Q5: Multi-metric ensemble performance**
- The ensemble approach doesn't outperform the best individual metric, which I also flagged in my review.

Now I'll prepare my responses to these points.
```

{
  "text": "\n## Reviewer 2 对 Reviewer 1 审稿意见的逐条回应\n\n---\n\n### Critical Weaknesses\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N1: 32B 模型样本量严重不足**\n同意这是一个严重问题。n=19-20 的 test set 使 bootstrap CI 过宽（Appendix Z.2: MDE 8.6-9.5pp），validation set ~4 traces per class 的超参选择本质上近似随机。论文的 exact permutation test（Section D, exhaustive enumeration of 2^n permutations）在统计方法上是小样本的 gold standard，但这只解决了 p-value 的可靠性，不解决 effect size estimation 的精度问题。\n\n补充发现：论文在 Appendix Z.2 的 post-hoc power analysis 中诚实报告了 32B 的 MDE 远高于 7-8B（Table 39），但 Contribution 1 的叙述（Section 1）依然将 32B 的结果作为 \"metric choice reverses conclusions\" 的关键证据——如果 32B 的 HS 优势本就在 noise floor 内，则 \"reversal\" 可能只是 statistical noise。R1 建议将 32B 降级为 \"illustrative\" 是合理的。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N2: 架构多样性不足**\n同意。Table 1 显示 R1-8B (Llama 8B), OT-7B (Qwen 7B 28 layers), QwQ-32B (Qwen 32B 64 layers), R1-32B (Qwen 32B 64 layers)。3/4 模型基于 Qwen 架构。论文虽声称 \"cross-architecture evidence\"（Section 1），但 Llama 仅有一个 8B 模型，无法排除 crossing-rate shallow bias 是 Qwen embedding normalization 或 attention pattern 的特性。\n\n补充：不过论文在 Appendix S (Encoder Robustness) 中展示了 HS 优势在 BGE-large encoder 上的一致性（Table 24-25），这提供了一些 encoder-independence 的证据，但仍不解决 base model architecture 的多样性问题。R1 建议修正措辞为 \"two architecture families\" 是精确的。\n\n**[REBUT] R1-N3: Multiple testing burden 被严重低估**\n部分不同意 R1 的严重程度判断。\n\n理由：\n1. 论文的 ~2,240 configurations 是 hyperparameter search space（Section 4.1: 5 metrics × 7 windows × 25-64 layers），但 HB 校正的 12 个 tests 并非从中 cherry-pick 的。论文在 Section 4.1 脚注明确说明：nested CV 在 validation set 上选 layer/window，然后在 held-out test set 上评估。这是标准的 train/validation/test split 流程，不是 post-hoc selection。\n2. Appendix W (Hyperparameter Robustness, page 28) 展示了 full exploration 的 effective degrees of freedom 远小于 2,240——因为 threshold-based metrics 在不同 configurations 下收敛到相似的 layer（Table 30: BA-optimal layers span 24 layers but mean BA landscape peak ratio 1.67）。\n3. Appendix W.1 (Nested Cross-Validation, page 28) 在 7-8B 上验证了 layer selection stability：10/10 folds favor HS (Table 31)，shrinkage relative to holdout estimates 仅 13% 和 25%。\n\n但 R1 有一个有效的子论点：nested CV 无法在 32B 上执行，而 32B 恰恰是最需要 robustness 验证的模型。这与 N1 形成 compounding 问题。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N4: Position residualization 揭示 QwQ-32B 问题**\n同意这是一个重要发现。Appendix V, Table 29 显示 QwQ-32B 在 quadratic residualization 后 HS 优势从 +1.4pp 降至 -1.0pp（线性）和完全消失。这意味着 QwQ-32B 的 HS probe 可能主要在 exploit 位置信息。\n\n补充：但需要区分模型。Table 29 同时显示 R1-8B (+10.4pp → +10.5pp) 和 OT-7B (+1.4pp → +1.4pp) 在 residualization 后优势几乎不变，说明位置伪影是 QwQ-32B specific 而非 systematic。R1-32B 在两种规格下均不显著，但鉴于 N1 的样本量问题，这可能是 power 不足而非真正的 null effect。\n\n论文确实在 Section V 讨论了这一点（\"3 of 4 models retain non-positional signal\"），但我同意 R1 的判断：Abstract 和 Discussion 应更显著地反映 QwQ-32B 的位置伪影问题，而不是将其埋在附录中。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N5: R1-32B control task selectivity低**\n同意。Hewitt and Liang (2019) 的 5pp 阈值是该领域的标准基准，R1-32B 仅 3.0pp（Section H, Table 10, page 20）。结合 N1（n=20）和 N4（residualization 后不显著），R1-32B 的全部结论确实应标记为 \"inconclusive\" 而非 \"exploratory\"。\n\n补充：论文在 Appendix D (Holm-Bonferroni step-down correction, Table 10) 中报告 R1-32B 通过了 HB 校正（12 tests 全部 pass），但 control task selectivity 是一个独立的 validity check，与统计显著性不同。一个 statistically significant 但 selectivity 不足的 probe 可能在学习 surface patterns。\n\n**[REBUT] R1-N6: Crossing-rate shallow bias 是 trivially expected**\n部分不同意。\n\n理由：\n1. \"Shallow layers 有更小的 cosine distance\" 确实是已知的 transformer 几何性质，但论文的贡献不在于 document 这个性质——而在于展示 **这个性质通过 crossing-rate metric 传导到 layer selection，从而系统性地影响 downstream probing conclusions**。这是一个 causal chain，即使每个环节都是 \"known\"，完整链条的 empirical demonstration 仍有价值。\n2. 论文在 Appendix C (Representation Compression Analysis, Table 8, page 15) 提供了定量证据：shallow layers 的 step-to-step cosine distance 为 0.04-0.19（mean 0.186, std 0.097），deep layers 为 0.09-0.41——crossing-rate inflation 在 shallow layers 是 1.4-2.7×。这个量化不是 trivial。\n3. Random-label control（Section B.1）的 100% shallow selection 确实强化了 bias 论点，但 R1 的替代解释（shallow = more noisy → random success）需要更多证据支持。论文的解释（geometric bias independent of label）更 parsimonious。\n\n但我也承认：如果一个 experienced probing researcher 看到 \"crossing rate selects shallow layers\"，他们可能会说 \"当然如此\"。论文需要更好地 frame 为什么这个 \"expected\" 结果在 safety context 下 **matters**——因为 downstream practitioners 可能并不了解 probing 方法论文献。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N7: 单数据集限制泛化性**\n完全同意，这也是我 Review 中的 N2。补充一点：不同 harm categories 的 CP 分布可能差异很大。论文在 Section 9 (Limitations) 提到 HarmThoughts 涵盖 violence, fraud, illegal activities 等，但 \"we do not stratify results by category\"。如果某些 categories 的 CP 系统性地更早或更晚，layer selection sensitivity 的严重程度可能 category-dependent。\n\n---\n\n### Questions\n\n**[AGREE] R1-Q1: QwQ-32B HS probe 本质上是 position detector？**\n如 N4 回应所述，Table 29 的 quadratic residualization 证据支持这一担忧。但需注意：(a) 这是 QwQ-32B specific，R1-8B 和 OT-7B 不受影响；(b) 位置信息本身可能与 safety deliberation 有 genuine correlation（更长的 trace 可能意味着更多 deliberation），所以 \"position detector\" 不完全等于 \"spurious\"。不过论文确实需要更明确地讨论这一点。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-Q2: 为什么 nested CV 没有在 32B 上执行？**\n论文在 Appendix W 中隐含解释了原因（n=19-20 不足以支撑 nested CV 的内外层 fold），但应在主文中明确说明。这与 N1 是同一根本问题（数据量不足）。R1 建议使用 LOO 是一个合理的替代方案，虽然 LOO 在 metric selection 上仍有 high variance 的问题。\n\n**[REBUT] R1-Q3: Random-label 100% shallow 是否只因 shallow 更 noisy？**\n不同意 R1 的 alternative interpretation。\n\n理由：\n1. 如果 shallow layers 只是 \"more noisy\"，那么 random labels 应该在所有 layers 上表现同样差（因为 label 与 representation 无关）。Crossing-rate 在 shallow layers 更高不是因为 noise，而是因为 step-to-step cosine distance 更小（Table 8），导致更多 threshold crossings——这是 deterministic geometric property，不是 random noise。\n2. 论文在 Section B.1 的分析明确指出 \"the crossing-rate shallow-layer preference is label-independent\"，这正是 geometric bias 的定义：选择与 task 无关，仅由 representation geometry 驱动。\n3. 如果 R1 的解释成立（shallow = noisy → any metric works），那 threshold-based metrics 也应该 prefer shallow layers——但 Table 2 显示 threshold-based metrics 一致选择 deep layers，这直接反驳了 \"noise\" 解释。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-Q4: HarmThoughts CP annotation 无 IAA**\n同意。论文在 Limitations (Section 9) 明确承认 \"inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed\"。CP 与 chain length 的相关性是一个重要的 confound——如果 CP 位置与 trace length 高度相关（如 CP 总是在 trace 的 60-70% 处），那 CP-based 分析可能只是在 proxy trace length。论文在 Section 9 提到 \"most CPs occur in the first half of reasoning\"，但未给出 CP/length 相关性的量化分析。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-Q5: Multi-metric ensemble Ens-Best 不显著**\n同意，这也是我 Review 中 N3 的核心论点。Appendix Z.1, Table 40 显示 Ens-Best Δ 在 -3.9pp 到 +3.3pp 之间，p>0.27。Ensemble 仅在 Ens-Text 维度上显著（+10.5pp 到 +27.0pp），但这本质上只是说 \"HS ensemble 比 text baseline 好\"——这不是新信息。论文需要更诚实地承认：当前提出的 mitigation（multi-metric ensemble）并未真正解决 metric selection 问题。\n\n---\n\n### Missing References\n\n**Nanda et al. (2023) \"Progress Measures for Grokking\"**: 该论文研究 grokking 的 mechanistic interpretability，与本文的 probing layer selection 主题关联较弱。可以不引用。\n\n**Gurnee et al. (2023) \"Finding Neurons in a Haystack\"**: Sparse probing 方法论与本文的 linear probing 相关，但 focus 不同（neuron-level vs. layer-level）。可作为 related work 的补充但非必须。\n\n**Li et al. (2024) \"Geometry of Categorical and Hierarchical Concepts\"**: 与 representation geometry 相关，可在讨论 shallow vs. deep layer 表征差异时引用，但同样非必须。\n\nR1 建议的 3 篇 missing references 都不是 must-cite。论文现有的 78 条引用已经覆盖了核心相关工作。\n\n---\n\n### 与我的 Review 的交叉对照\n\nR1 和我在以下问题上 **完全一致**：\n- 单数据集限制（R1-N7 ≈ 我的 N2）\n- 缺少 actionable solution（R1-Q5 ≈ 我的 N3）\n- 统计严谨性是论文亮点（R1-S2 ≈ 我的 S1）\n- Negative results 报告诚实（R1-S3 ≈ 我的 S4）\n- Overall 3.0 / Findings 级别\n\nR1 提出了我未覆盖的重要问题：\n- **32B 样本量问题（N1）**: 我仅在 S1 中 positively 提及了 exact permutation test，但未充分关注 effect size estimation 的精度问题。R1 的分析更到位。\n- **架构多样性（N2）**: 我未明确指出 3/4 Qwen 的问题。这是一个有效的补充。\n- **Position residualization（N4）**: 我未深入分析 QwQ-32B 的位置伪影。R1 对 Table 29 的解读是正确的。\n\n我提出了 R1 未覆盖的问题：\n- **CP 的 binary formulation 过于粗糙（我的 W1）**: R1 未讨论 CP 定义本身的问题。\n- **写作层面的 hedging 过度（我的 W4）**: R1 未评论 \"retrospective characterization\" 的反复强调。\n- **Section 编号错误 5→7（我的 W2）**: R1 未注意到此格式问题。\n",
